| MYSQLD-HYPOTHESES | **RANKED, 2026-10-16 — nothing applied, nothing restarted; every test names what confirms it, what KILLS it, its cost, and whether it is the developer's call because it changes the machine.** **H1 — the Aria control/log file HANDLE is invalidated after startup by something outside mysqld (a filter driver: Defender real-time scanning the data directory; or a copy/restore over the directory while mysqld ran).** Fits: EBADF nine minutes into a pristine directory, EINVAL on every later checkpoint, both directories, healthy disk, no Aria setting touched. Confirm: Process Monitor (Sysinternals, read-only trace, admin) filtered on `aria_log_control` and `aria_log.*` — the failing `WriteFile` with its result and every other process that opens, deletes or renames those paths; and the admin read `Get-MpPreference \| select ExclusionPath` (is `C:\xampp\mysql\data` excluded? today unknown). Kill: a trace that shows mysqld's own write failing with no other process ever touching the file. Cost: 30 min of trace during one suite run. Machine change: NO for the trace; adding a Defender exclusion to test it is a change — developer's. **H2 — the kill-stop pattern: XAMPP's Stop is `killprocess.bat`, 10 of 11 starts since 14/10 recovered from a crash, two mysqld processes started 5 s apart on 15/10; Aria's control file is left half-written and each start inherits a worse state.** Fits: the history of Aria recoveries at every dirty start since July; the 10-05 failure identical to today's. Does NOT by itself explain EBADF nine minutes after a clean first start. Confirm: a week of `mysqladmin -u root shutdown` only, with the startup list — zero recovery lines and zero `checkpoint failed` lines. Kill: a `checkpoint failed` after a start that followed a clean stop (today's log already shows one such case: 16:41:52 clean start → 17:13:51 Errcode 22, 32 minutes later — so H2 alone is already weakened). Cost: nothing but discipline. Machine change: no. **H3 — a defect in this MariaDB 10.4.32 Windows build's Aria file handling (XAMPP 8.2.12 bundle).** Fits: the errors are Aria-specific, repeat across directories and months, on a healthy volume, with default Aria settings. Confirm: the same install's behaviour on another machine, or the MariaDB tracker for `checkpoint failed` + `Errcode 9/22` on Windows 10.4; kill: H1's trace showing an outside writer. Cost: 1 h reading. Machine change: no — but the remedy (upgrading or replacing the server) is one. **H4 — our two my.ini changes.** Killed by timing: the first Aria error (16:35:21) precedes the buffer-pool change (16:41:52), and `skip-grant-tables` only affects privilege checks; neither touches Aria. Cost: 0. **H5 — disk or filesystem.** Weak: SSD Healthy, NTFS Healthy, no System-log storage events. Confirm/kill as admin: `Get-StorageReliabilityCounter`, `fsutil dirty query C:`, `chkdsk C:` WITHOUT `/f` (read-only scan). Cost: 5 min, admin. Machine change: no. **H6 — Windows Search / sync agents.** Killed today for Search (`C:\xampp` in no crawl rule) and for sync agents (none running, none scoping the path); Defender remains under H1. **THE OPTION NOBODY WOULD PUT ON THE LIST BY DEFAULT, stated plainly: this MariaDB under XAMPP is not worth stabilising.** The evidence points there — Aria has recovered or failed at least nine times since July in two directories on a healthy disk, the only stop button is a kill, and the engine that keeps failing is the one that holds `mysql.plugin`, so one bad log record takes the whole server down. **Moving the dev database:** (a) MariaDB 11.x as a Windows SERVICE (installer, `net stop` is a clean shutdown; keep `127.0.0.1:3306`, import the dev database, point `core/.env` at it): ~2–3 h including the import and one suite run; (b) Docker Desktop `mariadb:11` with a bound volume: ~2 h, needs Docker on the machine, adds a daemon; (c) WSL2 + MariaDB: ~3 h, two filesystems to reason about. All three remove the XAMPP control panel from the stop path and replace the 10.4.32 binary; all three are machine changes — **the developer decides, and it is on the list.** Recommended order if nothing is decided today: H1's ProcMon trace during the inventory run (it is the one measurement that separates H1 from H3 and costs no change), the admin reads under H1 and H5, then the move if H3 stands. **2026-10-16 evening, after the run: the trace could not be made (no Process Monitor on the machine, shell not elevated), so H1 vs H3 is still undecided by trace. What the day added: crash #4 — an `ibdata1` page physically written into `storefront_payment_methods.ibd` (the file read from disk, not a server message) — i.e. a MISDIRECTED WRITE, the same class as the Aria control-file EBADF/EINVAL; this strengthens the I/O-path family (H1 or H3) and weakens the data and disk families further. **THE ONE RECOMMENDATION, with a cost: move the dev database off XAMPP's MariaDB 10.4.32 to MariaDB 11.x installed as a Windows SERVICE on this machine (~2–3 h: installer, keep 127.0.0.1:3306, import `eleganceeg-20261016.sql`, one gate run).** Reasoning from the evidence: the faults are I/O-path faults inside mysqld's process (wrong-file writes, bad handles), the hardware and filesystem are healthy, and the stop path is a kill by design; a service removes the kill path and replaces the binary, and if the fault FOLLOWS the new server on the same machine the trace question (Defender) is answered by that fact alone — the move is itself the H1/H3 experiment, and it is the cheaper one. Michael decides.** **2026-10-16 18:51 — H1 UNDER TEST: the developer put a Defender exclusion on `C:\xampp\mysql\data` (18:51), stopped pid 5924 with `mysqladmin` (18:51:10) and started pid 12572 (18:53:09).** What the logs hold (RUN_20261016.md rows 7–7e): the SHUTDOWN of the pre-exclusion process carried the fault one more time — `Aria engine: checkpoint failed`, EBADF on `aria_log_control` ×3 — and left `mysql.db` marked crashed; the new process started with BOTH clean-stop signatures on the InnoDB side (no crash-recovery line, `ib_buffer_pool` loaded, LSN continuous) and auto-repaired `mysql.db` (668 wrong-CRC pages; CHECK TABLE now OK with a size warning); **since 18:53:10, silence** — no Aria line, no Errcode, through a full gate run. Two readings of the 18:51:10 fault are on the run log: by the letter an Aria Errcode after 18:51 (the brief's STOP, taken); by the mechanism a stale handle discovered at shutdown, which does not by itself say the exclusion failed. **The exclusion cannot be read unelevated**, so it is recorded as supplied. The test that settles it: a clean stop and re-start repeating both signatures with no Aria line, then days of silence. Until then the move-to-service recommendation stands as written. **2026-10-16 evening — THE WATCH RULE CORRECTED (developer): only an Aria error or Errcode from a mysqld whose handles were opened AFTER the exclusion counts — pid 12572 (18:53:09) onward.** EBADF discovers an already-broken handle; pid 5924's handles predate the exclusion by 85 minutes, so the 18:51:10 shutdown lines are old damage found at flush time on the same file that failed 16 times before. **The first encouraging measurement:** before the exclusion Errcode 22 appeared 14 times, every one during a suite run; after it, three full gate runs under pid 12572 (19:22, 576 s; 19:58, 527 s; 20:15, 507 s) produced nothing — not proof, one process lifetime. | VARIANT-MATRIX-RULE | **DECIDED 2026-10-16 (developer) — spec §17.5: the COLOUR PICKER is its own new-work row, and the MATRIX RULE is written.** A variant is ONE row of `catalog_product_variants` carrying colour AND size with its own stock in both buckets and its own `price_delta`; the PDP therefore offers the rows that exist and nothing else — choose a colour and only its existing sizes are enabled (a sold-out size struck, not hidden), choose a size and the colours narrow the same way — **never two independent pickers**, which would offer a combination with no row (the hole) or sell a colour whose every size is out (the dead colour); the price is the chosen row's (v2 `products/{id}` emits it per row); add-to-cart carries `variant_id`; a product whose rows carry only sizes shows the size picker alone, only colours the colour picker alone. The colour picker: 24px swatches, radius 2, `edge` ring, `role=radio`, checked = 2px ink ring, the name beside the chosen one; it reads the variant rows' `color_id`, never `catalog_product_color` (that is the card's descriptive row — SWATCHES-ON-CARD; the filter's swatches are `role=checkbox`). **🔴 The core finding behind the rule (code read 2026-10-16; file and line in B3-GROUND-20261016): the LIVE storefront path cannot sell a variant product at all.** Compat emits no variants and no delta, refuses one at the cart and at checkout (422 at both doors, by design since wave 3.5), and `CompatVariantInvariantTest` holds that no storefront-1 product has variants; only the v2 API carries variants, and `Frontend-next` never calls it for products. Not decided here. | GATE-VIRTUAL-CHECKSUM | **Line 4 of the suite gate can trip WITHOUT a write — found 2026-10-16 on the demo run, measured, not fixed (the finding is the deliverable).** The gate reported `storefront_category_product` CHANGED (CHECKSUM TABLE 924012449 before → 2130778793 after). **Every one of its 14,525 rows is identical to the production export it was imported from** — compared row by row, every column, time zone aligned (`D:\eleganceeg-20261016.sql` holds the table's 21 INSERT statements; 0 ids differ, 0 rows differ). So no test wrote to it. The table is the ONLY one in the schema with a GENERATED column — `primary_guard` bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS (CASE WHEN is_primary = 1 THEN product_id END) VIRTUAL, under the unique key `scp_one_primary_unique` — and the same movement is in an earlier run's files today (OaQof0, a 59/59 run: 770294931 before → 2130778793 after); every AFTER value today is 2130778793 and CHECKSUM TABLE answers 2130778793 repeatedly now. **Hypothesis, stated as one:** MariaDB 10.4.32's CHECKSUM TABLE hashes the record buffer including the VIRTUAL column, whose bytes are not deterministic until the rows have been read (the suite reads them), so the BEFORE value flakes and the AFTER value settles — not confirmed against a bug id. **What to do (developer's call):** make the guard's checksum content-only for tables with generated columns — for this table `SELECT COUNT(*), SUM(CRC32(CONCAT_WS('|', id, storefront_id, storefront_category_id, product_id, sort_order, is_primary, IFNULL(created_at,''), IFNULL(updated_at,''))))` answers 14525 / 31251224184563 twice in a row — or build every table's column list from information_schema excluding `IS_GENERATED = 'ALWAYS'`; until then a line-4 trip on this one table is read against the export before it is called a leak. The two 59/59 gate runs recorded as "0 tables changed" happened to read 2130778793 before and after. **✅ CLOSED 2026-10-16 evening — the content-only checksum is IN `suite-baseline.sh` and PROVED on both halves (RUN_20261016.md row 8b):** on a scratch copy one cell (row 1 `sort_order` 0 → 7) moved the table's sum 31251224184563 → 31251392300435 and the guard named exactly that table, the revert returned the value, the untouched table beside it did not move; on the dev database two passes 24–25 s apart differed on 0 of 139 tables with `storefront_category_product` at 31251224184563 both times. `CHECKSUM TABLE` is out of the gate; `--checksum DB OUTFILE` is the diagnosis mode. | before the next gate run | Dev. |

| A2-RULE | **Architecture — two layers, built differently (settled before 2026-10-13; recorded 2026-10-13 because it existed only in chat).** **The core is one factory:** one codebase serves both shops, and inside it everything that differs between the shops is DATA — the storefront row, its settings, its assets, its copy — never a code path; `if (storefront is brandfashion)` in the core is the signal to stop. **The frontend is not one app:** each storefront is its own folder, its own codebase, its own build, its own deploy. Brand Fashion gets a new folder built for Brand Fashion — not `Frontend-next` serving two hosts with a theme, and no runtime "which storefront am I" question anywhere in it. What is shared is everything the shopper does not see — data handling, the cart, the payment path, SSR, how data is stored, split and linked — and it gets there by being COPIED from Watchizer into Brand Fashion's folder; copied, not shared at runtime. Two reasons: the designs are not variants of each other (Brand Fashion's nav is its own component on its own identity, not Watchizer's recoloured — true of every visible piece); and a runtime switch makes every visitor pay for both shops, whereas separate builds ship each visitor only their own. Michael's framing: one chocolate — same raw material, same factory, same proportions — sold as two brands, one shaped and wrapped restrained and modern, one loud and irregular; the factory is the core and the proven functionality nobody sees, the chocolate is the products, the shaping and wrapping is the storefront. Rejected: standing at the counter holding raw material, asking the customer "refined or loud?" and making them wait. **Accepted cost, recorded as a cost:** a copy means a fix in Watchizer does not reach Brand Fashion. Accepted, but only safe while visible: when the frontend work starts, propose how the copied files are tracked so that touching one says it has a twin — proposed then, not designed or built now. **Changes nothing about current work:** A2, W2, W3/W4, C10 and step D are all core. Its visual identity is already built (§22). The three rules stand as rules: differences are data, not code paths; no per-storefront branching for presentation in the API or business logic; a genuine BEHAVIOUR difference is named and stopped at, never built around or assumed. Recorded here and as BF handover D13 so it is met wherever A2 is read. Core identity comes from the storefront row/settings, never a constant and never the request host; every asset per storefront with its path recorded; a storefront with no asset is a named failure, consistent with `StorefrontHasNoHost` — never a fallback to Watchizer's wordmark, never a silent blank. | A2, the app build | — |

| SF-URL-SETTINGS | **Pre-existing gap, found 2026-10-12 while building A2, NOT introduced by it:** no dashboard field writes any of the four per-storefront URL settings (`storefronts.settings.urls.{frontend,payment_return,asset_base,api_host}`); they are set by SQL when a storefront is created. **Decided (developer, 2026-10-12): SQL only for A2** — a screen for a one-time value is not worth a session inside work that is already long. **Consequence, spelled out:** at step D someone runs SQL against production for Brand Fashion, so the statement is written here ahead of time, not composed on the night. **The temporary hosts, DECIDED 2026-10-13 (developer): `michaelkhalaf.com` and `api.michaelkhalaf.com`** — the apex, chosen so the `api.<domain>` pattern holds and step D stays a one-line domain update (`StorefrontHost::storefrontIdFor()` matches exact or sub-domain, so `api.michaelkhalaf.com` resolves to the row that says `michaelkhalaf.com`); `bf.michaelkhalaf.com` is retired from every statement here. Since the hosts follow the pattern ONLY the domain changes and every URL derives: `UPDATE storefronts SET domain = 'michaelkhalaf.com', updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = 2 AND code = 'brandfashion';` If any host does NOT follow it, set the setting too — **statement REWRITTEN 2026-10-13 (developer hit it locally):** the earlier form was `JSON_SET(settings, '$.urls.api_host', …)`, and `JSON_SET` adds a member to an EXISTING object but does not create a missing intermediate level, so on a row whose `settings` has no `urls` key (Brand Fashion's, which holds `promotions` and `identity`) it answered "Query OK, 1 row affected" and changed nothing — a silent no-op on exactly the row it was written for (proved on local MariaDB 10.4: `JSON_SET('{"identity":{…}}', '$.urls.api_host', 'x')` returns the input unchanged; the identity statements were never affected because `$.identity` is one level deep). The form that creates the object and keeps every sibling key: `UPDATE storefronts SET settings = JSON_MERGE_PATCH(COALESCE(settings, JSON_OBJECT()), JSON_OBJECT('urls', JSON_OBJECT('api_host', 'https://<the API host>', 'asset_base', 'https://<the API host>', 'frontend', 'https://<the site>', 'payment_return', 'https://<the site>/'))), updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = 2 AND code = 'brandfashion';` — the `COALESCE` is load-bearing: `JSON_MERGE_PATCH(NULL, …)` is NULL and would blank the column. **Then the check that names failure, because "1 row affected" is not proof a JSON path landed:** `SELECT id, JSON_EXTRACT(settings, '$.urls') AS urls, IF(JSON_EXTRACT(settings, '$.urls.api_host') IS NULL, 'FAILED: urls did not land', 'OK') AS landed FROM storefronts WHERE id = 2;` — `urls` must not be NULL and `landed` must read OK. Then clear the storefront caches (`php artisan cache:clear` — the hosts and URL rows are cached for `storefront.ttl.storefront`, 600 s) and prove with `php artisan tinker --execute="echo App\Storefront\StorefrontUrls::apiHost(2), PHP_EOL;"`. Phase F runs the same with the real domain. **Why step D is not cosmetic (2026-10-13):** with identity set, `StorefrontIdentity::for(2)->wordmarkUrl()` answers `https://api.brandfashionegy.com/assets/brand/brandfashion-mail-wordmark@2x.png` — a host that is not ours and does not resolve — so every Brand Fashion mail rendered today carries a dead image; the URL derives from the asset host, which derives from the domain, so step D moves it to `https://api.michaelkhalaf.com/assets/brand/brandfashion-mail-wordmark@2x.png` (expected, not a bug), and the verification after step D must print that URL and `curl -sI` against it must answer `200` `image/png` once that host serves. **The statement lives in `core/docs/deploy/sql/step-d-local.sql`** — a FILE fed to the client (**developer, 2026-10-13: the file stays OUT of git** — `.gitignore` line 10 excludes `*.sql` for good reasons and no exception is carved; so its text is HERE, version-controlled, and the file is re-created from it: `UPDATE storefronts SET domain = 'michaelkhalaf.com', updated_at = NOW() WHERE id = 2 AND code = 'brandfashion' AND domain = 'brandfashionegy.com';` then `SELECT id, code, name, domain, is_active, settings, updated_at FROM storefronts WHERE id = 2;` — domain only, the guard names the old domain so a second run changes 0 rows, then `php artisan cache:clear`. The developer's LOCAL row still says `bf.michaelkhalaf.com` (2026-10-13); the local file's guard is `domain IN ('brandfashionegy.com', 'bf.michaelkhalaf.com')` so the same file moves either state and a second run changes 0 rows), because a statement typed into PowerShell is escaped by the shell (an identity statement handed on 2026-10-12 would have written a literal `\$` as a JSON path); the shape that is safe is `Get-Content step-d-local.sql | mysql -u <user> -p <database>` or `mysql ... < file`. The suite survives it: `StorefrontUrlsTest` "survives step D" moves the row inside a transaction and proves every URL follows (2026-10-13; the forty-eight pinned `brandfashionegy.com` literals now come from the row through `Tests\Support\Shops`, the twenty-three that remain are the OLD WordPress site's address in the import, archive-slug and TLS tests and a CORS parsing string — not the row's). **The dashboard-fields decision was REVERSED on 2026-10-13 — see SF-SETTINGS-SCREEN (built the same night; SQL stays the path for the first production run).** | Step D (and F) | minutes |
| SF-SETTINGS-SCREEN | **REVERSAL, recorded as one (built 2026-10-13, overnight brief §4.1).** **What was decided before:** SF-URL-SETTINGS, **2026-10-12** — the domain, the four `settings.urls.*` overrides and (with W2) the five `settings.identity.*` values are set by SQL only; "the dashboard fields can come when a THIRD storefront makes them worth it". **What is decided now, 2026-10-13, and why:** they are set on the storefront's settings screen, `/manage/storefronts/{id}/edit` — ONE screen, the existing one, with three sections added (Addresses, E-mail identity, Mailbox). The reason is the statement handed over on 2026-10-12 for the identity: typed into PowerShell it would have written a literal `\$` as a JSON path (`JSON_SET(settings, '\$.identity', …)`), and a value only a developer with a database client can set is a value the shop cannot correct on a Friday evening. **SQL (now a file, `core/docs/deploy/sql/step-d-local.sql` and the SF-IDENTITY-SQL statements) stays the path for the FIRST production run**; after it, the screen. **Validated BEFORE the write** (`StorefrontSettingsForm`): the conditions `StorefrontHasNoHost` refuses at send time (no domain → `api_host` demanded, and for a non-default shop `frontend`/`asset_base` too; every override `https://`, host-only except the payment return, no query, trailing slash dropped; the domain a bare lower-case hostname) and the ones `StorefrontHasNoIdentity` refuses (wordmark path a PNG that EXISTS under `public/`, checked against disk; both WhatsApp numbers 10–15 international digits; copyright not empty) become form errors — a save that passes is a save the first order after it will not fail on. The mailbox section validates SHAPE (reserve < cap, every recipient an e-mail) and reports usability as `missing` on the page, so half a mailbox can be saved on Monday and the password on Tuesday. The password is write-only (blank = keep; `password_set` is the only thing the page is told; the activity log stores a marker). **Saving clears the cache, proven:** `StorefrontSettingsScreenTest` primes `StorefrontUrls`, `StorefrontIdentity` and `StorefrontMailer`, saves, and reads the new values with no `cache:clear` (the existing `forgetStorefront()` plus `StorefrontMailer::forget()`). **Who can reach it:** `manage-storefronts` holders (admins; no data-entry grant includes it), within their storefront scope — a Brand-Fashion-scoped admin edits Brand Fashion and gets 404 for `/manage/storefronts/1/edit`; data entry 403; a guest the sign-in page. **One consequence to know:** clearing the domain of a shop with no URL override is now REFUSED (it would have no API host) — four SITES in `StorefrontScreenTest` posted `domain: null` incidentally and now post `watchizereg.com`: three test bodies ('refuses a default locale that is not one of the enabled locales' — asserts the `default_locale` error and that the column is unchanged; 'never lets the form rename the storefront code' — asserts `code` stays `watchizer`; 'records the settings change, and never copies the settings blob into the log' — asserts the log row's author, label, storefront and that `settings` is not snapshotted) and the `storefrontPayload()` helper shared by the four money-switch tests (the switch on/off reaching the engine, written as a real boolean, other settings keys kept, admin-only) — seven tests through four sites. None of them existed to prove a blank domain is accepted; `null` was there because the field was `nullable` and the test was about something else, so no premise changed (checked line by line, 2026-10-13; the developer asked because of the `StorefrontIdentityTest` trap). **Arabic path, measured 2026-10-13:** the seam, like every dashboard screen — each `t()` in `Edit.tsx` carries its Arabic literal as the fallback (61 calls, 39 added by §4.1, 0 without Arabic), each of the controller's `ManageText::t()` messages likewise (16, 10 added, 0 without), and Laravel's rule messages come from `lang/ar/validation.php`; `lang/ar/manage.php` stays the 1,027-byte stub pinned by `LocaleSeamTest`; `TranslationCoverageTest`, `ServerTranslationCoverageTest` and `ValidationLocaleTest` hold the bargain and are in the full run. A request that sends none of the three sections changes none of them (the pre-§4.1 form still works). Measured: `StorefrontSettingsScreenTest` 7, `StorefrontScreenTest` 15, `StorefrontCacheInvalidationTest` green; Pint, PHPStan, `tsc` clean; ~50 English strings added to `lang/en/manage.php` under `storefronts`. | Deploy (with A2) | — |
| ACTIVITY-SEARCH | **✅ BUILT 2026-10-13 (overnight brief §4.2).** **The table's shape, read first:** `core_activity_log` (id, user_id, user_name, subject_type, subject_id, subject_label, action, storefront_id, changes JSON, created_at) with three indexes — `(created_at)`, `(user_id, created_at)`, `(subject_type, subject_id)`. **What existed already:** filters by user, action, subject type and date range, the filters in the URL (`DataTable` rebuilds every page link from `meta.filters`/`meta.search`, so they survive paging — now PROVEN by a test: page 2 of a filtered 30-row set holds the last five), a CSV export of the current view, and a free-text box that was `LIKE %term%` over two UNINDEXED columns — a full scan. **Built:** (1) migration `2026_10_21_000000_activity_log_indexes` (M2h): `(action, created_at)`, `(storefront_id, created_at)`, `(subject_label)`, `(user_name)`, `(subject_id)` — guarded by name, additive; (2) the search is a PREFIX match (`LIKE 'term%'`) on `subject_label` OR `user_name`, plus `subject_id =` when the term is all digits; the plan on the live local table is `index_merge` over TWO of the FIVE new indexes — `activity_label_idx` and `activity_user_name_idx`; | C-GROW | **🟠 Contained — prune BUILT 2026-10-09; the fix is C-NARROW. The file cache grew without limit.** Measured on production by the developer 2026-10-07: `storage/framework/cache` = **343,699 files / 7.6 GB** in 9 days; live generation ~972 files. **Mechanism (code, 2026-10-09):** every key embeds the storefront version (`StorefrontCache.php:157, 244`); a dashboard save (`ProductWriter::flush`, lookups, placements, tree, banners) or a stock movement (`StockChanged` → `FlushStorefrontCachesOnStockChange`) bumps it with one `Cache::increment`; the request's `terminating` warm (`AppServiceProvider.php:218–229`) writes a whole new generation under the new key; the old one is never read again, and Laravel's `FileStore` deletes an expired file **only when that key is read** (`FileStore.php:414–418`) — so it stays for ever. (The 5-minute `catalog:warm` rewrites the SAME keys in place: no growth from it.) **Growth from production data** (`wz_prod_20261008`, `cache-bumps.sql`): **411 bumping requests** 2026-09-28 → 10-07 = **44/day** (10–100; dashboard saves, not shopper orders — 3 system stock movements in the period); one Watchizer generation measured locally = **978 files / 11.1 MB** → **~43,000 files and ~0.5 GB a day** today. **Reading corrected 2026-10-09:** the developer's hourly mtime histogram (37,150 files / ~0.8 GB a day) counts **dead generations, not writes** — the live generation's files are rewritten in place every 5 minutes, so their mtime always reads "now" and they never appear in a past hour; each past hour shows only the generations stranded in it (the larger bytes per file: earlier generations carried the retired whole-catalogue file — the census, when run, says whether those are in the 7.6 GB). **Brand Fashion:** every product is placed on both shops, so a bump flushes BOTH; with BF warmed one bump writes **8,740 files / 48.5 MB** (BF generation measured: 7,762 files / 37.4 MB) → **~385,000 files / 2.1 GB a day at 44 bumps, ~874,000 / 4.9 GB on a 100-bump day**. **Inode headroom (2026-10-07, developer): 516,539 of 2,000,000 used → 1,483,461 left**, shrinking ~43,000/day from Watchizer alone; with BF warmed that is **~3.6 days at the average rate, ~1.6 days on a busy day**. **Built (developer's go 2026-10-09, option (a); (b) rejected):** `php artisan cache:prune-expired [--force]` (`app/Support/Cache/ExpiredCachePrune.php`) deletes only files whose own expiry stamp is more than 1 h past (`--grace`), never a forever entry, never a non-sha1 file name or a file outside the cache directory, and never a LIVE-generation file — refused by file name (sha1 of the current version's keys), whatever its stamp — then re-checks every live file; read-only without `--force`. Scheduled (`routes/console.php`) **nightly 03:25 while only Watchizer is warmed, hourly at :25 as soon as `COMPAT_WARM_STOREFRONTS` lists a second storefront** — the cadence follows the setting. Tests `tests/Feature/Cache/ExpiredCachePruneTest.php` (6; the live-file refusal mutation-proven). One-time cleanup for the 7.6 GB already there: `new branding/scripts/cache/cache_expired_cleanup.php` (same guards, read-only unless `CACHE_CLEANUP_DELETE=1`) or the command once deployed. **THE EVIDENCE — one-time cleanup run on production by the developer 2026-10-09:** `cache_expired_cleanup.php` deleted **410,077 files / 8,192.9 MB**; **1,019 files / 278 MB** left on disk. The guard held: **996 of 996** live Watchizer files and **1 of 1** Brand Fashion file still present, confirmed by the script's own re-check. The census (run first) answered the open question: the retired L8 whole-catalogue families WERE still on disk — `compat_all_product` **350 files / 2.75 GB** and `compat_all_product_image` **100 files / 84 MB** — so `new branding/scripts/l8/l8_cache_cleanup.php` never ran on production (the expired-file cleanup removed them with the rest; it is not owed any more). The problem was real at this scale: 8.2 GB and ~410,000 inodes of dead generations against a 2,000,000-inode quota, — up from 343,699 files / 7.6 GB measured on 2026-10-07, two days earlier. **C-WARM measured 2026-10-09 (interval stands). The prune only CONTAINS the growth; C-NARROW is the fix, C-WARMSKIP cuts the no-op warms** | Built — deploy before BF warm-up / domain | S (prune) — done |
| C-NARROW | **DEFERRED — an EFFICIENCY item, not a safety one (developer, 2026-10-11).** First production prune, 2026-10-11 03:25: **38,467 files examined, 37,447 / 422 MB cleared, protected=1010, failed=0, ok=yes**. The criterion set on 2026-10-10 ("due if daily churn runs to tens of thousands") is met — but the prune's cadence switches to HOURLY by itself once a second storefront is warmed (`routes/console.php`), which caps the intra-day peak, so the inode risk is contained either way. The real cost is churn: **~422 MB written and deleted a day today, roughly 3.7 GB a day once Brand Fashion is warmed** (×8.9, the generation-size ratio below). Leave deferred. Original scope: **the actual fix for C-GROW — scoped, NOT built (developer 2026-10-09: "the prune is containment").** Today any bump makes a storefront's WHOLE generation stale: one product save or stock movement re-writes ~978 Watchizer files, and **8,740** once Brand Fashion is warmed (C-GROW). Narrower invalidation = a write stales only what it changed: the product's own `compat_card` (and `product`) per storefront it is on — `forgetCard`/`forgetProduct` already exist — plus the listing index and nav, which every product change does affect: **~3 files per bump instead of 8,740**. The version bump stays only for writes that genuinely change everything (lookups, tree, banners, storefront settings). Design questions for its own session: which writers move off `flush()` (ProductWriter, StockChanged listener, placements), whether the index/nav are versioned separately from the cards, what `INVALIDATION_MAP` and `CacheInvalidationMapTest` then assert, and the warm-on-write half (rebuild 3 entries, not a generation). Once built, the prune becomes a backstop | Its own design session | M |
| C-WARM | **✅ MEASURED 2026-10-09 (developer, on the host) — the 5-minute interval stands.** Watchizer `catalog:warm` on the host: **1.663 s real** (index rebuild 1,285 ms) against 4.7 s locally → the host is **~2.8× faster**. Brand Fashion's warm locally: 96 s = 20.4× Watchizer's local. **ESTIMATE, not a measurement:** Brand Fashion on the host **~34 s, ~11% of each 5-minute cycle** (the earlier "a third of every cycle" came from the local figure and was wrong). The real figure is taken the day `COMPAT_WARM_STOREFRONTS=1,2` is switched on (`time php artisan catalog:warm --storefront=2` — after `config:cache`, see the config-cache trap). The work the warm repeats for nothing is C-WARMSKIP | Closed — re-measure at switch-on | — |
| C-WARMSKIP | **Scoped, NOT built (developer 2026-10-09).** The warm runs **288×/day**; the version bumps **~44×/day** — and every bump is already followed by a warm-on-write (`AppServiceProvider` `terminating`). So ~244 scheduled runs a day rebuild an unchanged generation: **~2.3 h of CPU a day** once Brand Fashion is warmed (244 × ~34 s, C-WARM's estimate). Distinct from C-NARROW (that shrinks what a bump writes; this cuts how often the warm runs) — they compound. **What it actually takes, read from the code:** (1) **The TTL is the catch.** The warm writes every entry with `compat.ttl.all_product` = **600 s**; a skipped tick lets the generation EXPIRE 10 minutes later and the next shopper pays the cold build — the thing the warm exists to prevent. So a skip only pays if version-keyed entries live much longer (e.g. 24 h — the version, not the TTL, already retires them). Longer life means dead generations wait longer for `cache:prune-expired` (TTL + 1 h grace), raising peak files on disk (C-GROW: ~385,000 files/day with BF) — size it against the inode headroom, or land C-NARROW first. Never `forever`: the prune refuses forever entries by design. (2) **Not everything bumps.** A new rating only `forgetCard()`s (K6) — the INDEX's rating and the rating sort refresh only via the warm; `adjustOffer()` changes offer stock without `StockChanged`. Either those writers mark the storefront for a re-warm (a `sf:{id}:dirty` flag, or a bump), or a skipped generation serves them stale for up to the TTL. Order stock DOES bump (`InventoryService::adjust` → `StockChanged` → flush). (3) **The marker:** `sf:{id}:warmed` = `{version, shape, at}`, written by `CompatListing::warm()` only after the LAST card chunk is stored (an interrupted warm leaves no marker), with the version read at the warm's START (as `refresh()` does — a bump during the warm makes the next tick run). Written by both the scheduled warm and warm-on-write. Stored in the file cache with a finite TTL longer than the entries' (so it is prunable); `cache:clear` deletes it → the next tick warms — fail-safe. Skip only when: version == marker's, `shape` == the code's current fingerprint, `at` younger than TTL − 2 ticks, AND the index key is on disk (`Cache::has` — a prune, expiry or manual delete cannot leave a phantom "warm"). (4) **First run after a deploy:** the version counter survives a deploy (forever key) but a deploy can change what a card or the index CONTAINS without a bump — today the 5-minute rewarm is what puts the new shape on disk. So `shape` must be a fingerprint of the build (e.g. a release id written at deploy, or `INDEX_SHAPE` + a builder version constant): a new fingerprint ≠ marker → the first tick after the deploy warms in full, then skips resume. Without it, a deploy's catalogue change would sit unseen until the next bump. (5) Tests: skip on an unchanged version; run after a bump, a rating, an offer-stock change, a `cache:clear`, a deleted index file and a new fingerprint; marker absent after an interrupted warm | After BF warm-up; with or after C-NARROW | S–M |

The media buyer's products reached Meta only through "catalog from pixel" (built from what the pixel happened to see — most without images). `GET /feeds/meta/{storefront}/{token}.csv` on the API host serves a file `php artisan feeds:meta` writes hourly at :40 (`withoutOverlapping`; temp file + rename; replaced only when the content changed, so the ETag is stable; a 304 on a matching validator in the code and its tests — not delivered on production, FEED-304). **Never built on a request** (a missing file is a 404; tested with the generator unbindable). `config/feeds.php` keyed by storefront code: token (`FEED_META_WATCHIZER_TOKEN`, 40 letters/digits, `hash_equals`), domain written in config (not `storefronts.domain` — E3), image host, locale `en`, `exclude` (empty on purpose). No entry or an empty token = no feed and a 404, never another storefront's. Meta's field limits are checked and REPORTED, never cut. Generated from the warm's listing index + 500-product chunks (`productRows`/`productImages`) — no whole-catalogue build. **Measured on `wz_prod_20261008` at the host's real `memory_limit` 3072M:** 975 products, 890,629 bytes; cold index 757–792 ms, peak **48 MB = 1.6 % of the host's 3072M `memory_limit`** (measured on the host twice, CLI and web, 2026-10-07); index already warm (separate process, as on the host) 444–449 ms, peak **46 MB = 1.5 % of 3072M** (the local warm itself peaks 48 MB measured the same way; host warm 25.8 MB = 0.8 %). The ceiling is written next to every figure on purpose: a memory number without its ceiling once became a launch blocker that did not exist (L8). Stock split: 66 express, 809 market only, 100 out of stock; 0 values over a limit. 16 tests (`tests/Feature/Feeds/MetaFeedTest.php`) + the allow-list case. **Allow-list line (runbook §4.1.1) added by hand at the deploy, then the `.htaccess` backup re-taken** — the one way this feed fails silently. | FEED-304 | **The feed's 304 does not reach production (measured by the developer 2026-10-10) — bandwidth only, not urgent, not a rollback.** Live: correct token → 200 with `etag: "d600958bd1d3efb654dc299e4d54df1d79879a6e"` (the file's sha1 digest), `cache-control: no-cache, public`, `last-modified: Sat, 10 Oct 2026 12:09:21 GMT`; `If-None-Match` with that exact quoted ETag → **200**; `If-Modified-Since` with that exact date → **200**. Cost: up to 891 KB an hour, ~21 MB a day. **One symptom, TWO separate bugs (measured 2026-10-10 — not one cause, as first reasoned):** (1) `If-Modified-Since` → 200 was the CODE — the controller never read it; (2) `If-None-Match` → 200 is NOT reproducible off the CDN: the same controller behind a local HTTP server answered **304** to the exact ETag. Production sits behind **Hostinger's CDN** (`server: hcdn`, `x-hcdn-cache-status: DYNAMIC`), which rewrites validators: on the public `/api/v2/watchizer/meta` it serves a WEAK `W/"…"` ETag uncompressed and a strong one with `br`, and answers 304 to a `W/` value that core's exact-match PHP compare would reject — so that 304 comes from the CDN, not PHP. Whether the CDN does not forward `If-None-Match` for the feed or forwards it altered is undecided until one request skips the CDN (the origin probe below). **Also fixed in code (2026-10-10, small and safe):** the controller now answers by HTTP's rules via Symfony's `isNotModified()` — weak comparison (`W/` matches), a list, `*`, and `If-Modified-Since` when there is no `If-None-Match`; a test covers each, run against the old code it fails. **What the original test asserted, and the gap:** an in-process request carrying the exact strong ETag got 304 — the controller's compare, with no web server and no CDN in between; it could never see what reaches PHP on production. **Origin probe (developer, on the host, skips the CDN):** `curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}
' -H 'Host: api.watchizereg.com' -H "If-None-Match: $E" "https://127.0.0.1/feeds/meta/watchizer/$T.csv"` — 304 = PHP receives the header and the CDN is what drops the 304; 200 = the header does not reach PHP through LiteSpeed. | PERF-HERO-CHUNK | **🔴 DEFECT — FIXED.** **✅ DEPLOYED 2026-10-11 (developer)** — in the same push as PERF-LOGO (`5925c2c`, `c73530a`, `25a842f`), whose live AVIF (7,773 B) the developer verified; deployment of THIS change is inferred from that build, not separately proven live (the live 3/3 chunk-failure test is offered, not run).  If the 3D hero's lazy chunk fails to load, the WHOLE home page showed the error screen ("This page is temporarily unavailable") instead of the poster: the ChunkLoadError is thrown by the `next/dynamic` component itself, OUTSIDE `WatchCanvas`, so its own `ModelErrorBoundary` (which lives inside the chunk that failed) never sees it and the error reaches `app/(main)/error.jsx`. Causes: a flaky connection, a blocker cancelling the request, a page whose hero mounts after a deploy removed its chunk. **Reproduced deliberately** on a local production build (`next start`, core down — the home renders without it, rails show their retry), Playwright routing the five files `react-loadable-manifest.json` lists for `WatchHero.jsx -> ./WatchCanvas` (none is in the home's initial load): aborted, 404 and empty-200 responses. Before: 3/3 the error screen; control (nothing blocked) renders the canvas. **Fix:** `HeroCanvasBoundary` in `WatchHero.jsx` (the home page's own chunk) around `<WatchCanvas>`, rendering nothing on failure so the poster stays. After (fresh build): 3/3 the home page with its headline and the poster visible, no error screen; control still mounts the canvas. Cost: home route 6.91 → 7.03 kB, First Load JS unchanged (173 kB); lint warnings unchanged (37). Lands on both shops. Not covered: a failure AFTER the model has shown leaves the poster hidden (not reproducible on purpose, so not built). | Deployed 2026-10-11 | — |

A route's JavaScript chunk failing on a client-side navigation shows the error screen (reproduced: footer About link with `/about-us`'s page chunk aborted or 404, 2/2). **The tab left open across a deploy is NOT this case — Next already handles it, measured:** Next 15.5 compares the build id on every route fetch and does a full page load on a mismatch (`fetch-server-response.js`); local "deploy" test — three tabs on the home page of build A (About prefetched / not prefetched / visited and left), server stopped, build B made with a one-string change in `TrustPage.jsx` (reverted after the build; new build id), started, then About clicked in each old tab: **3/3 full page loads onto build B** (new markup present, old tab marker gone), 0 error screens. Every page route here is dynamic, so no 5-minute static prefetch cache can bypass the check (only the two icons are static). **The defect that remained:** after a chunk failure, "Try Again" NEVER recovered — `reset()` re-renders the same failed load (measured: network restored, two clicks, still the error screen; same in the auth boundary). **Fix:** `src/utils/chunkError.js` — on a chunk-load error "Try Again" does a full page load; every other error keeps `reset()`. Wired into `app/error.jsx`, `app/(main)/error.jsx`, `app/(auth)/error.jsx`. **No loop by construction:** the reload exists only in the button's click handler — no automatic reload, no session flag needed. Measured on a fresh build, before → after: network back, one click — error screen → About page (1 page load); auth boundary — error screen → login page (1 page load); chunk STILL failing, one click → the About page from the server HTML (its own client code absent), no error screen; fully offline, one click → the browser's own connection-error page; **0 page loads in the 20 s after, without clicks, in every case**; a non-chunk error (product page, core down) still calls `reset()`, same document, unchanged. Route sizes and lint warnings unchanged; the PERF-HERO-CHUNK repro still passes. Trade-off: fully offline, the visitor sees the browser's page instead of ours (our "Back to Home" was a full page load too). Both shops. | Deployed 2026-10-11 | — |

The same build locally (sharp 0.35.5) re-encodes: 64,610 B → 11,786 B AVIF. A Next 15 server that cannot load `sharp` serves the original — so the host's runtime most likely has no working `sharp`. **Possibly a regression of today's storefront deploy** (`52771de` moved sharp 0.35.4 → 0.35.5 and its binaries): a desktop Lighthouse run about an hour earlier still got the logo optimised at w=256 (6.7 KiB) — an older cached result. | GATE-WIDTH-20261019 | **🔴 WHAT THIS EPISODE ACTUALLY WAS, recorded because the next person may not stop.** On the morning of a production deploy the suite gate read **40 regressions on a tree that was green**, and the deploy was paused at `RUNBOOK_BF_LAUNCH.md` §1.2 — correctly, because that is what the gate said. **The gate's verdict depended on the width of the operator's terminal.** `suite-baseline.sh` compares test names scraped from Pest's printed `FAILED` lines against `tests/baseline/suite-baseline.txt` by exact string equality; Pest fits each line to the terminal and cuts the name with an ellipsis, so 40 of the 61 baseline entries are STORED TRUNCATED at 105–106 characters (the width the file was written at, 120 columns). On a 187-column console those 40 names print whole, match nothing, and are counted as NEW failures in section 2 **and** as newly-PASSING in section 3 — the same 40 tests in both lists, which is impossible and was the tell. Measured: at `COLUMNS=80` the name is 66 characters, at 120 exactly the baseline's 106, at 400 the whole 139. **No regression existed:** both of the developer's runs and the agent's had the same totals (61 failed / 39 skipped / 2064 passed) and the same 61 failing tests, and every baseline entry maps to exactly one of them and back — 0 unmatched in either direction. The agent's runs were green only because its shell had no `COLUMNS` and the default here resolves to 120, the width the baseline happens to have been written at. **A gate that can cry wolf is worse than no gate**: it trips on green code, it trips differently for different people, and the pressure at that moment is to assume the tooling and carry on. Fixed by GATE-WIDTH-PIN; properly fixed by GATE-JUNIT-NAMES. | recorded | — |
| GATE-WIDTH-PIN | **✅ DONE 2026-10-19 (the developer chose it, one line).** `suite-baseline.sh` line 99 runs the suite as `COLUMNS=120 php artisan test …`, which overrides whatever the console reports, so the names the gate compares are the same for every operator and for CI. The baseline file was NOT touched — the fix makes the run match the file that already existed, rather than bending the file to pass. **Proved by THREE FULL GATE RUNS, at outer console widths 80, 187 (the developer's) and 400** — every one green, NEW 0, stale 0, CHANGED 0, `mysql.db` OK either side, and in every one the suite printed at **inner width 120, longest name 106, 40 truncated, the failing set equal to the baseline exactly**: the outer width no longer reaches the comparison. The developer's own failing log, replayed through `--log`, reproduces the trip (21 known / 40 new / 40 stale, exit 1), so the before and the after are both on record. **Do not remove the prefix** while the gate still parses human output. | done | — |
| GATE-JUNIT-NAMES | **🟠 THE REAL FIX — required BEFORE the gate ever runs on another machine or in CI.** Stop parsing output meant for people: run the suite with `--log-junit` and read the XML, whose `<testcase classname=… name=…>` carries the FULL name and can never be truncated, then regenerate `suite-baseline.txt` with full names (keeping each entry's cause text and its `[demo-on]` tag). GATE-WIDTH-PIN only makes the current rendering deterministic; it leaves the gate reading a display format that Pest is free to change in any release, and leaves the baseline unreadable (40 of its 61 entries end mid-sentence). **The collision risk it also removes, recorded:** two tests whose "Class > name" agree for the first 105 characters truncate to the SAME baseline entry, so one of them can fail silently inside the other's known-red line — none do today (all 61 stored names are distinct, and the longest untruncated is 105), but nothing prevents it, and the names in this suite are long sentences. **Until GATE-JUNIT-NAMES lands, add the cheap guard with it or before it:** a check in `suite-baseline.sh` that fails when two entries of the failing set share a truncation prefix, or when two baseline entries are equal after truncation — loud, not silent. **Cost:** about 1–2 hours — change the extraction, regenerate the 61 entries, prove the regenerated set maps one-to-one onto today's failing set (the comparison that diagnosed this episode is exactly that proof), then run the gate twice: once for green, once with a deliberate break to show it still trips. | before CI or a second machine | M |

By the rule that removed InstaPay these are not true for Brand Fashion yet. Not removed: whether Brand Fashion takes cards is the Paymob contract, the developer's. Its PRODUCT page is unaffected (BfProductPage prints its own promises, which are true). **✅ DECIDED 2026-10-19 by the developer: LEAVE them** — Brand Fashion takes cash only until the Paymob contract, and the badges stay as they are until it does. **On the pre-launch checklist as P13** (`RUNBOOK_BF_LAUNCH.md` §9): re-check on launch day — if cards are still not accepted then, the two badges come off; when the contract is live they are already true. | decided — re-check at launch (P13) | XS |

## SEO. Audit of the live storefront, 2026-09-27 (read-only; M = measured, I = inferred)

**RESOLVED 2026-09-28: Googlebot is NOT blocked** — the developer's Search Console check shows `https://watchizereg.com/` indexed and available. What follows is kept as the record. **Before anything else — the bot wall (M, cause; I, effect on Google).** Hostinger's CDN answers
any request that asks for compressed content (`Accept-Encoding`) with a 403 "Checking your
browser" challenge, whatever the user agent — which is why Lighthouse/PageSpeed get 403 and plain
curl gets 200. Real Googlebot is PROBABLY let through by IP (Hostinger's docs), but that cannot be
proven from outside. **Owner check (minutes):** Search Console → URL Inspection → Test live URL on
the home page and a product; Settings → Crawl stats → 403 count. If Googlebot is refused, turn off
the bot protection / whitelist verified bots in hPanel → CDN — nothing below matters until then.

| # | Finding | Fix | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|

**Owner decision (not an open item):** several products are priced far below genuine retail while
every page and description says "Authentic, certified & guaranteed". Whether those claims stand is
the client's call, raised with them by the developer (2026-09-27). It matters before any Google
Merchant Center feed (counterfeit / misrepresentation policies), which also requires the trust
pages that currently 404 (about, contact, privacy, terms, returns).

### S-AR — Arabic for Google, in stages that each ship

Decision to take first: **English keeps the bare URLs** (they carry today's ranking and links) and
Arabic moves under `/ar/…` — the reverse of the v2 sitemap's current default-locale-unprefixed
rule, which must be flipped for Watchizer.

   **Stage 1 BUILT 2026-10-01 (overnight, in the tree, storefront only, not deployed).** How it works:
   - **Middleware** (`Frontend-next/middleware.js`) rewrites `/ar/…` onto the existing routes and
     marks the request Arabic. Visiting an `/ar` URL sets the `wz-lang=ar` preference.
   - **Bare URLs are unchanged:** the cookie decides what a person sees, and the metadata is
     always the English page's. Google has no cookie, so it sees English on bare URLs and Arabic
     under `/ar`.
   - **The UI store is now per request** (`src/Store/uiStore.js`, same `useUIStore` API), started
     in the request's language. Before, zustand rendered the server HTML from the store's initial
     state, always `'en'`, so Arabic existed only after mount.
   - **Metadata in the URL's language, self-canonical, with hreflang en/ar/x-default:** home,
     product (+ its JSON-LD and breadcrumbs), `/listing`, brand, category, grade, subtype and
     suptype/brand pages. The Arabic home copy is the root layout's own Arabic defaults.
   - **The language switch moves `/x` ↔ `/ar/x`.** Redirects (`/products/{id}`, non-canonical
     slugs, offers) keep the `/ar` prefix.
   - **Verified.** A local HEAD build was diffed against the new one on 8 English pages: the ONLY
     change is the three hreflang tags. The home page's featured block is random per request, so
     its text differs regardless. Arabic pages carry Arabic title, description, H1, JSON-LD and
     breadcrumbs, as a Googlebot user agent sees them. In the browser, filters on `/ar/listing`
     keep the prefix and the switch round-trips; the home/product/cart/facet checks pass in both
     languages.
   - **Not done, noted:**
     - (a) Links INSIDE `/ar` pages are still bare. Shoppers stay in Arabic by cookie, but a
       crawler following them lands on English. Stage 3's sitemap and the hreflang tags cover
       discovery; prefixing internal links is a follow-up.
     - (b) Offer pages keep English metadata (there are 0 offers).
     - (c) `/listing`'s metadata is streamed into the body even for Googlebot. That's Next's
       handling of a page that reads query parameters; it was the same before stage 1.
1. **Arabic pages that exist for Google (4–6 days).** `/ar/…` for home, product, category/brand
   facet pages and listing, served by a middleware rewrite onto the existing routes with the locale
   passed down. The server renders Arabic — the server catalogue already carries `productsAr` — and
   the client store starts from the URL's language, not only after mount (the cookie becomes a
   preference for the language switch, not the source of truth). Per-locale `<title>`, description,
   H1, alt text and JSON-LD names from the Arabic title + short description (≈100% filled, so no
   hand-written meta needed); self-canonical per language; hreflang ar/en/x-default on every page;
   the language switch links between the two URLs. **Buys on its own:** every product and category
   becomes indexable in Arabic — the Arabic queries that are most of Egyptian search volume.
   Browser pass in both languages (RTL), and a check that the English pages are byte-for-byte
   unchanged apart from the hreflang tags.
   **Stage 2 BUILT 2026-10-01 (overnight, in the tree, not deployed).**
   - **`catalog:clean-meta-titles`** (dry run by default; `--apply` writes, activity-logs per product
     and forgets that product's cached detail). On the dev copy: 380 titles on 380 products, all
     English, e.g. "Hugo Boss Watch For Men 1513755 | Select…" → "Hugo Boss Watch For Men 1513755".
     **NOT applied to the dev DB** (tested in a rolled-back transaction). Run it on production as a
     deploy step.
   - **The rule** (`MetaText::title`): the last " | " segment goes when it ends in "…" or "...". A
     tail that ends in a word (" | Diver") stays.
   - **Found while wiring it:** 215 stored titles are copied already cut off mid-word ("… Blue
     Dial Silver ..."). The data keeps them. `MetaText::usableTitle` treats a title ending in
     ".."/"…" as absent, so the page falls back to the product's full title.
   - **`catalog/product` now also returns `meta`** (cleaned title and description per language),
     beside the rows, which keep the legacy shape.
   - **The product page (my call, overrule if you like):**
     - It uses the team's meta title when it has 20+ characters, plus " | Watchizer". A bare
       model code like "ar11348" doesn't count.
     - It uses the team's meta description first.
     - Otherwise it keeps TODAY's chain (the price-led description, then the short description,
       then the template), in each language. The plan above had the short description ahead of
       the price-led text. I kept the price-led text as the fallback, because it carries the
       price and it's what every product has today; only products with written meta text change.
   - **Arabic meta is empty everywhere** (0 of 698), so Arabic pages use the Arabic templates
     from stage 1.
   - **Category/brand meta is empty everywhere** (0 of 44 in either language), so the facet
     pages keep their templates.
2. **Metadata quality (1–2 days).** Clean the " | Select…" junk out of `meta_title` (one-off
   command, dry run first); wire `meta_title`/`meta_description` with the fallback
   meta[locale] → title[locale] + brand, and meta_description[locale] → short_description[locale]
   → template. **Buys:** better snippets and click-through in both languages.
3. **Sitemap per language (1–2 days).** Serve the v2 per-locale sitemaps (flipped prefix rule) with
   `xhtml:link` alternates; fold in S5's dead-URL fixes; image host = the host pages use. **Buys:**
   faster, complete discovery of the Arabic URLs, and no 404s in the sitemap.
4. **Optional, later: Arabic slugs** (`/ar/product/ساعة-…`) and Arabic landing copy for
   categories/brands. Marginal ranking gain, real redirect/duplicate risk — decide after stage 1's
   Search Console data.

Total ≈ 1.5–2.5 weeks, stage 1 alone ≈ one week. Prerequisite for all of it: the bot-wall check
above.

## C. Operations and security — no code, or not ours

| # | Item | What it costs | What breaks if never done |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Rotate `JWT_SECRET` on BOTH hosts — the public-key half DROPPED 2026-09-28 (see START HERE).** ~~Rotate `JWT_SECRET` and the storefront's public API key~~ — the only two values pasted into a chat. **Paymob is NOT part of this:** nothing exposed its credentials (corrected 2026-09-24; an earlier note here listed it by mistake) | Every customer is signed out (tokens last 30 days). While legacy is up it must change on BOTH hosts at once; **after C3 it is core alone — simpler.** The public key means panel + `.env.production` + core's `COMPAT_API_KEY` together, then a rebuild (a short window of 401s) | **Anyone holding `JWT_SECRET` can mint a valid token for ANY customer id** — full access to that account's profile, addresses and orders. This is the one item whose risk is not cosmetic. Recommend days, not next season |
| C2 | **SHIPPED in batch 1.** **Next.js 15.5.21 → 15.5.26** (critical RCE advisory GHSA-2xp9-vwfh-vxw4, fixed in 15.5.24) + `sharp` 0.35.3 → 0.35.4 | A lockfile refresh, a build, a visual check. Behaviour changes: AVIF *sources* served unoptimised; AVIF *output* quality rescaled (80 → 50, 70 → 44, 90 → 56 — smaller, slightly softer). Cheap hardening in the same build: remove `127.0.0.1:8000` and `localhost:8000` from production `remotePatterns`, and the Farfetch host if nothing uses it | Exposure is narrow — our uploads are re-encoded to WebP, so no outsider can place an AVIF on our hosts — but an allowed third-party host or a future upload path turns it into unauthenticated remote code execution on the storefront server |
| C3 | **Switch the legacy storefront off** (§4A.4) — after any card payment started there has settled | Small. It also retires C4 and the undeployed `backend/` fixes, and makes C1 single-host | Legacy keeps answering; anyone who reaches it writes orders into the database nobody reads any more |
| C4 | **CLOSES WITH C3 (2026-09-28).** **The legacy gate question** — `dash.watchizereg.com/api/catalog/meta` answered 200 without `Api-Code` | Two reads (cache-busted curl; the key's length in legacy's cached config) | Moot once C3 is done. Until then, if the gate is open, runbook §9.1A's residual-risk row is wrong as written |
